# Consideration of funding proposals - Addendum XIII

## Funding proposal package for FP238

### Summary

This addendum contains the following seven parts:

1. **A. Funding proposal titled "Ecosystems-based Adaptation for resilient Watersheds and Communities in Malawi (EbAMI)"**
2. **B. No objection letter issued by the national designated authority(ies) or local authorities.**
3. **c) Environmental and social report(s) of disclosure;**
4. **d) Secretariat's assessment;**
5. **e) Response from the accredited entity to the independent Technical Advisory Panel's assessment; and**
6. **f) Gender devaluation.**

# Green Climate Fund Funding Proposal V 3.0 | Page 8

## Contents

### Section A: Project / Programme Summary
- Brief overview of the project or programme

### Section B: Project / Programme Information
- Detailed description of the project or programme, including its objectives and scope

### Section C: Financing Information
- Details about the funding sources and their contribution to the project or programme

### Section D: Expected Performance Against Investment Criteria
- Description of the expected outcomes and performance metrics for the project or programme

### Section E: Logical Framework
- A logical framework outlining the relationships between inputs, activities, outputs, and outcomes

### Section F: Risk Assessment and Management
- An assessment of potential risks and a plan to mitigate them

### Section G: GCF Policies and Standards
- Information about the Green Climate Fund's policies and standards that apply to the project or programme

### Section H: Annexes
- Additional information, such as supporting documents or references

## Note to Accredited Entities on the use of the funding proposal template

Accredited Entities should provide summary information in the proposal with cross-references to annexes. Updates to the funding proposal and/or annexes will be provided in the funding proposal. Proposals exceeding the prescribed length will not be assessed within the usual timeframe. The recommended font is Arial, size 11.

3. **Use Emphasis**: Use asterisks (*) or underscores (_) to emphasize certain words or phrases. For example, "This is an important point" would be written as "**This is an important point**".

1. The document is a proposal for funding from the Green Climate Fund.
2. The proposal aims to address climate change impacts on major crops and livestock.
3. The proposal includes several key points:
   - Climate change will have significant impacts on crop yields, food security, and livestock production.
   - Major crops such as maize, rice, sorghum, and soybeans are expected to be affected by climate change.
   - Livestock production is also expected to be impacted by climate change, particularly in tropical regions.
4. The proposal includes several specific actions that will be taken to address these impacts:
   - Developing and implementing climate-resilient crop varieties.
   - Improving irrigation systems to help crops cope with changing weather patterns.
   - Promoting sustainable farming practices to reduce greenhouse gas emissions.
5. The proposal also includes a section on the potential benefits of addressing climate change impacts on major crops and livestock:
   - Increased crop yields and food security.
   - Improved livestock production and nutrition.
   - Reduced vulnerability to climate-related disasters.
6. The proposal concludes with a call for action, urging the Green Climate Fund to provide funding to support these efforts.

10. Climate change is compounding other shocks and stresses on food security. The impact of climate change on agriculture is expected to worsen in the coming years due to increased frequency and severity of extreme weather events, rising temperatures, and changes in precipitation patterns. These factors are likely to exacerbate existing vulnerabilities and create new ones for smallholder farmers, particularly those in developing countries.

11. Literacy rates in human development are low, both of which limit access to written information. The literacy rate among women is 65.9% and among men is 71.6%. The lowest rates were found in rural areas with less than 10% of the population having primary education.

12. Forests need sustainable management, not only for their roles in water-related ecosystems but also for mitigating climate change. The main crop, although it is not as drought tolerant as other crops under cultivation, such as sweet potato and maize, has been used to improve soil fertility and reduce erosion.

13. Watershed management and other adaptation measures have been scarce up to 2019. The adoption strategies for climate change adaptation in the face of climate change are still evolving, but there is a need for more research on how to effectively manage water resources in the context of climate change.

10. EDA supports adaptation to climate change through various strategies such as early warning systems, climate-smart agriculture, and integrated land use planning. These strategies aim to enhance the resilience of farmers and communities to the impacts of climate change. For instance, early warning systems can help farmers prepare for extreme weather events, while climate-smart agriculture promotes practices that reduce greenhouse gas emissions and improve soil health. Integrated land use planning involves coordinating land use activities with climate change adaptation strategies to ensure sustainable development.
21. The Project addresses the main barriers to successful adoption of ESA practices by emphasizing the importance of community engagement, capacity building, and policy support. Community engagement is crucial for ensuring that ESA practices are tailored to the needs and priorities of local communities. Capacity building involves providing farmers with the necessary knowledge, skills, and resources to adopt ESA practices effectively. Policy support is essential for creating an enabling environment for the adoption of ESA practices, including incentives, regulations, and institutional frameworks.
30. The Project also highlights the importance of monitoring and evaluation in assessing the impact of ESA practices on climate change adaptation. Monitoring and evaluation involve collecting data on the implementation and outcomes of ESA practices to inform decision-making and improve their effectiveness. This includes tracking indicators such as greenhouse gas emissions, soil health, crop yields, and water use efficiency.
31. The Project also emphasizes the need for a holistic approach to climate change adaptation that considers social, economic, environmental, and governance factors. A holistic approach recognizes that climate change impacts are complex and interconnected, and that addressing one factor may have unintended consequences on others. Therefore, it is essential to consider the needs and priorities of all stakeholders in developing adaptation strategies.
32. The Project also highlights the importance of integrating ESA practices with other climate change adaptation strategies such as disaster risk reduction, sustainable water management, and biodiversity conservation. Integrating ESA practices with these strategies can help to create a more comprehensive and effective approach to climate change adaptation.
33. The Project also emphasizes the need for research and innovation in developing new ESA practices that are tailored to local contexts and needs. Research and innovation involve exploring new technologies, methods, and approaches to climate change adaptation, and testing their effectiveness in real-world settings.
34. The Project also highlights the importance of building capacity for research and innovation in developing ESA practices. Capacity building involves providing farmers, policymakers, and other stakeholders with the necessary knowledge, skills, and resources to conduct research and develop new ESA practices. This includes training programs, workshops, and other educational initiatives.
35. The Project also emphasizes the need for policy support in developing ESA practices. Policy support involves creating an enabling environment for the adoption of ESA practices through incentives, regulations, and institutional frameworks. This includes providing funding for research and development, promoting public-private partnerships, and supporting the establishment of national and regional climate change adaptation strategies.

## Sub-Components and Villages Included in the Study Area

The study area includes waterheds and villages that are selected based on their vulnerability to climate change impacts. The sub-components will support the development of Adaptive Management Plans (AMPs), which are plans for macro-catchments contained in targeted sub-catchments. AMPs will be developed at the village level, with Sub-Catchment Action Plans (SCAPs) being plans for macro-catchments contained in targeted sub-catchments.

**Page 58**
---

127. One key benefit that the Project will generate is enhanced food security (SDG2 - Zero Hunger). EDA practices and knowledge transfer to local farmers could increase yields by more than 40%, under traditional management systems. In addition, interventions manifested in and deep-rooting EBA at national policies and investment plans will bring the EBA agenda at sectoral policies level – no massive scale impact and sustainability.

128. By scaling up EBA practices, the Project will generate environmental benefits in terms of better land and water management (SDG 13 - Climate Action). The integration of EDA into local plans could contribute to improving the enabling environment, and allow replication and scale-up of EDA through other projects and programmes. It is expected that projects such as the World Bank's "Livelihoods for All" project will be replicated in other countries.

129. Collective learning on EBA will be achieved by training not only the members of extended management units, but also farmers and local communities. The Project will facilitate knowledge exchange among stakeholders through regular meetings, workshops and seminars.

130. Sustainable adaptation to climate change is unattainable in interventions exclude the vulnerable groups or do not fully consider the needs of women. The vulnerable groups are characterized by lackant access to knowledge, resources and opportunities.

1. **Choose the Right Software**: Use OCR software like Adobe Acrobat, ABBYY FineReader, or online tools such as OnlineOCR.net. These tools can convert images of text into editable and searchable formats.

2. **Prepare the Image**: Ensure the image is clear and well-lit. Remove any background noise or distractions that might interfere with the OCR process.

4. **Run the OCR Process**: Feed the image into the OCR software and let it process the text. The software will convert the images of text into editable text.

5. **Review and Edit**: After the OCR process is complete, review the output for accuracy. Make any necessary edits to correct any errors or inconsistencies.

6. **Save the Transcription**: Save the transcribed text in a format that's easy to read and edit, such as Microsoft Word or Google Docs.

Remember, while OCR technology can be very accurate, it may not always capture nuances of language or formatting, especially in complex documents with tables or images. For professional results, consider using specialized software designed for document scanning and text recognition.

174. Gender Analysis: An analysis of the gender-related aspects of the project was also undertaken during the development of the GSMP and Gender Action Plan (GAP) (Annex 8). Although the Government of Malawi ratified most of the recommendations made by the UNFPA, including measures to reduce sexual violence against women and girls, gender inequalities are due to entrenched social norms including the co-ercive participation of men in household decision making processes.

1. The design of the proposed project has been carried out hand-in-hand with a National Project Preparation Task Team (CPPT) consisting of senior government staff and partner organizations. The CPPT is led by a senior government official who serves as the project manager. The CPPT is responsible for developing the project plan, including the objectives, scope, and timeline.
2. The proposed project aims to address the challenges faced by smallholder farmers in Malawi, including poverty, lack of access to credit, and limited market opportunities. The project will focus on improving the productivity and sustainability of smallholder farms through the adoption of modern farming practices and technologies.
3. The proposed project will be implemented in two phases. Phase 1 will involve the development of a comprehensive plan for the project, including the identification of priority areas and the selection of target groups. Phase 2 will involve the implementation of the project activities, including the provision of training and technical assistance to smallholder farmers.
4. The proposed project will be implemented in collaboration with various stakeholders, including government agencies, non-governmental organizations (NGOs), private sector companies, and local communities. The project will also involve the participation of women and youth in the implementation process.
5. The proposed project will be evaluated using a combination of quantitative and qualitative methods. The evaluation will focus on measuring the impact of the project on smallholder farmers' productivity, income, and sustainability. The evaluation will also assess the effectiveness of the project's implementation and identify areas for improvement.
6. The proposed project is expected to have a significant impact on the lives of smallholder farmers in Malawi. By improving their productivity and sustainability, the project will help to reduce poverty and promote economic growth in rural areas. The project will also contribute to the achievement of the United Nations' Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs), particularly SDG 7: Affordable and Clean Energy, SDG 12: Responsible Consumption and Production, and SDG 13: Climate Action.
7. The proposed project is subject to various conditions and requirements for funding. These include the submission of a detailed project plan, the provision of financial resources, and the implementation of the project activities in accordance with the approved plan. The project will also be evaluated based on its compliance with these conditions and requirements.

# II. Overall remarks from the independent Technical Advisory Panel

## I. Introduction

The Independent Technical Advisory Panel (ITAP) has reviewed the proposal "Ecosystems-based Adaptation for Resilient Waterbodies and Communities in Malawi" and provides its overall remarks. The ITAP believes that the project has been well planned, consulted, and designed by a team of experienced professionals with expertise in water resources management, climate change adaptation, and community engagement.

## II. Project Overview

The project aims to develop and implement an ecosystem-based adaptation strategy for resilient waterbodies and communities in Malawi. The strategy will focus on enhancing the resilience of these water bodies and communities to climate change impacts, such as flooding, drought, and heatwaves. The project will also aim to improve the livelihoods of local communities by providing them with access to clean water, improved sanitation facilities, and other essential services.

## III. Project Recommendations

The ITAP recommends that the board approve this funding proposal for the following reasons:

1. The project has been well planned and designed by a team of experienced professionals with expertise in water resources management, climate change adaptation, and community engagement.
2. The project has been designed to address the specific needs and challenges of resilient waterbodies and communities in Malawi.
3. The project has the potential to make significant contributions to the country's goals for climate change mitigation and adaptation.
4. The project has the potential to improve the livelihoods of local communities by providing them with access to clean water, improved sanitation facilities, and other essential services.

## IV. Conclusion

The ITAP recommends that the board approve this funding proposal for the "Ecosystems-based Adaptation for Resilient Waterbodies and Communities in Malawi" project. The project has been well planned, designed, and executed by a team of experienced professionals with expertise in water resources management, climate change adaptation, and community engagement. The project has the potential to make significant contributions to the country's goals for climate change mitigation and adaptation, as well as improve the livelihoods of local communities.

# Response from the accredited entity to the independent Technical Advisory Panel's assessment (FP230)

## Country: Republic of Malawi

## Project/programme site: Medium

## Impact potential

The AI would like to confirm that the detailed monitoring and evaluation (M&E) framework for the project is in full alignment with the logical framework and internationally recognised tools/guidance.

## Paradigm shift potential

Climate change/forest degradation and drought are major drivers of water scarcity in Malawi. The AI acknowledges that the overall sustainable development potential is assessed as high by ITAP.

## Needs of the recipient

The AI acknowledges that the overall needs of the recipient is assessed as high by ITAP.

## Country ownership

The AI acknowledges that the overall country ownership is assessed as high by ITAP.
